Appendix H
Proofs from §
4.2

H.1
Proofs from §
4.2.1

Proof H.1

Proof of Proposition
4.6
.
Let
\hat{b},\hat{w}
denote an optimal solution for (
BP
), and let the set
\widehat{\mathcal{T}}^{i}\triangleq\{t:\;\hat{b}^{i}_{t}=1\}
be defined for each sample path
i
. We will henceforth consider the optimization problem (
H
) in the case where the linear objective function
f(b,w)
is defined equal to the following function:

\displaystyle\hat{f}(b,w)\triangleq\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{t\in\widehat{\mathcal{T}}^{i}}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L^{i}_{t}-1}(\kappa^{i}_{\ell+1}-\kappa^{i}_{\ell})(b^{i}_{t}-w^{i}_{t\ell}).

We readily observe that the function
\hat{f}(b,w)
is linear in
b
and
w
. Moreover, it follows from algebra that
\hat{f}(b,w)
is less than or equal to the objective function of (
BP
) for all feasible solutions of (
BP
); that is, the linear function
\hat{f}(b,w)
satisfies the condition from line (
4
).
13
13

13

To see why
\hat{f}(b,w)
is a lower bound on the objective function of (
BP
), consider any arbitrary vectors
b,w
that satisfy the constraints of (
BP
). Since feasibility for the optimization problem (
BP
) implies that
b
is a binary vector, we observe that the equality
b^{i}_{t}(1-w^{i}_{t\ell})=\max\left\{b^{i}_{t}-w^{i}_{t\ell},0\right\}
holds for each
i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
,
t\in\{1,\ldots,T\}
, and
\ell\in\{1,\ldots,|\mathcal{K}^{i}|\}
.
Thus, we conclude that solving the optimization problem (
H
) with objective function
f(b,w)=\hat{f}(b,w)
will provide a lower-bound approximation of the optimization problem (
BP
), and any optimal solution for (
H
) will be a feasible solution for (
BP
). For notational convenience, we henceforth let
J^{\textnormal{\ref{prob:h}}}
denote the optimal objective value of (
H
) with objective function
f(b,w)=\hat{f}(b,w)
, and we let
J^{\textnormal{\ref{prob:bp}}}
denote the optimal objective value of (
BP
).

We first show that the optimal objective value of (
H
) with objective function
f(b,w)=\hat{f}(b,w)
is equal to the optimal objective value of (
BP
). Indeed, it follows from the fact that (
H
) and (
BP
) have the same constraints that the optimal solution
\hat{b},\hat{w}
for (
BP
) is a feasible solution for (
H
). Therefore,

\displaystyle J^{\textnormal{\ref{prob:h}}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{t\in\widehat{\mathcal{T}}^{i}}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L^{i}_{t}-1}(\kappa^{i}_{\ell+1}-\kappa^{i}_{\ell})\left(\hat{b}^{i}_{t}-\hat{w}^{i}_{t\ell}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{t:\hat{b}_{t}^{i}=1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L^{i}_{t}-1}(\kappa^{i}_{\ell+1}-\kappa^{i}_{\ell})(1-\hat{w}^{i}_{t\ell})+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{t:\hat{b}_{t}^{i}=0}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L^{i}_{t}-1}(\kappa^{i}_{\ell+1}-\kappa^{i}_{\ell})0

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L^{i}_{t}-1}(\kappa^{i}_{\ell+1}-\kappa^{i}_{\ell})\hat{b}^{i}_{t}(1-\hat{w}^{i}_{t\ell})

\displaystyle=J^{\textnormal{\ref{prob:bp}}},

where the first inequality holds because
\hat{b},\hat{w}
is a feasible but possibly suboptimal solution for (
H
), the first equality follows from the construction of the sets
\widehat{\mathcal{T}}^{1},\ldots,\widehat{\mathcal{T}}^{N}\subseteq\{1,\ldots,T\}
, the second equality follows from algebra, and the final equality follows from the fact that
\hat{b},\hat{w}
is an optimal solution for (
BP
). Since the optimal objective value of (
H
) is always less than or equal to the optimal objective value of (
BP
), we have proved that the optimal objective value of (
H
) is equal to the optimal objective value of (
BP
).

We conclude the proof of Proposition
4.6
by showing that every optimal solution for (
H
) with objective function
f(b,w)=\hat{f}(b,w)
is an optimal solution for (
BP
). Indeed, let
\bar{b},\bar{w}
denote an optimal solution for (
H
). Then,

\displaystyle J^{\textnormal{\ref{prob:h}}}=\hat{f}(\bar{b},\bar{w})\leq\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L^{i}_{t}-1}(\kappa^{i}_{\ell+1}-\kappa^{i}_{\ell})\bar{b}^{i}_{t}\left(1-\bar{w}^{i}_{t\ell}\right)\leq J^{\textnormal{\ref{prob:bp}}},

where the first equality follows from the fact that
\bar{b},\bar{w}
is an optimal solution for (
H
) and from the fact that the objective function of (
H
) is
f(b,w)=\hat{f}(b,w)
, the first inequality follows from the fact that the linear function
\hat{f}(b,w)
satisfies the condition from line (
4
), and the final inequality follows from the fact that
\bar{b},\bar{w}
is a feasible but possibly suboptimal solution for (
BP
). Since we have previously shown that the optimal objective value
J^{\textnormal{\ref{prob:h}}}
of (
H
) is equal to the optimal objective value
J^{\textnormal{\ref{prob:bp}}}
of (
BP
), we have thus proven that
\bar{b},\bar{w}
is an optimal solution for (
BP
). This concludes our proof of Proposition
4.6
.

\halmos

H.2
Proofs from §
4.2.3

Proof H.2

Proof of Lemma
4.9
.
Let
f(b,w)=\bar{f}(b,w)
, and consider any binary vector
b
that is optimal for the optimization problem (
H
). Since
b
is binary, we readily observe that there exists an optimal choice for the remaining decision variables
w
in the optimization problem (
H
) in which the following equality holds for each sample path
i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
, period
t\in\{1,\ldots,T\}
, and
\ell\in\{1,\ldots,|\mathcal{K}^{i}|\}
:

\displaystyle w^{i}_{t\ell}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}0,&\text{if }\left[b^{j}_{s}=0\textnormal{ for all }j\in\{1,\ldots,N\}\textnormal{ and }s\in\{1,\dots,t\}\textnormal{ such that }g(s,x^{i})\leq\kappa^{i}_{\ell}\textnormal{ and }\mathcal{U}^{i}_{s}\cap\mathcal{U}^{j}_{s}\neq\emptyset\right]\\
&\text{and }\left[b^{i}_{s}=0\textnormal{ for all }s\in\{1,\ldots,t-1\}\right],\\
1,&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

(62)

We will now construct a new solution for (
H
) that has the same or greater objective value than
b,w
. Indeed, let
\bar{b},\bar{w}
be a solution for (
H
) defined by the following equalities for each sample path
i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
, period
t\in\{1,\ldots,T\}
, and
\ell\in\{1,\ldots,|\mathcal{K}^{i}|\}
:

\displaystyle\bar{b}^{i}_{t}

\displaystyle\triangleq\begin{cases}1,&\text{if }\left[b^{i}_{t}=1\text{ and }t=T^{i}\right]\text{ or }\left[t=T\right],\\
0,&\text{otherwise};\end{cases}

\displaystyle\bar{w}^{i}_{t\ell}

\displaystyle\triangleq\begin{cases}0,&\text{if }\left[\bar{b}^{j}_{s}=0\textnormal{ for all }j\in\{1,\ldots,N\}\textnormal{ and }s\in\{1,\dots,t\}\textnormal{ such that }g(s,x^{i})\leq\kappa^{i}_{\ell}\textnormal{ and }\mathcal{U}^{i}_{s}\cap\mathcal{U}^{j}_{s}\neq\emptyset\right]\\
&\text{and }\left[\bar{b}^{i}_{s}=0\textnormal{ for all }s\in\{1,\ldots,t-1\}\right],\\
1,&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

We observe for each sample path
i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
that
\bar{b}^{i}_{T}=1
and
\bar{b}^{i}_{t}=0
for all
t\in\{1,\ldots,T\}\setminus\mathcal{T}^{i}
. Moreover, we readily observe from inspection that the solution
\bar{b},\bar{w}
is feasible for (
H
). Therefore, it remains for us to prove that
\bar{b},\bar{w}
is an optimal solution for (
H
).

To show that
\bar{b},\bar{w}
is an optimal solution for (
H
), we observe
for each sample path
i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
, period
t\in\{1,\ldots,T\}
, and
\ell\in\{1,\ldots,L^{i}_{t}-1\}
,

\displaystyle\bar{w}^{i}_{t\ell}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}0,&\text{if }\left[{b}^{j}_{T^{j}}=0\textnormal{ for all }j\in\{1,\ldots,N\}\textnormal{ such that }T^{j}\leq t,\;g(T^{j},x^{i})\leq\kappa^{i}_{\ell},\textnormal{ and }\mathcal{U}^{i}_{T^{j}}\cap\mathcal{U}^{j}_{T^{j}}\neq\emptyset\right]\\
&\text{and }\left[b^{i}_{T^{i}}=0\text{ if }T^{i}\in\{1,\ldots,t-1\}\right],\\
1,&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

\displaystyle\leq w^{i}_{t\ell},

(63)

where the equality follows from the definition of
\bar{b},\bar{w}
, and the inequality follows from line (
62
). Therefore,

\displaystyle\bar{f}(\bar{b},\bar{w})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}^{i}}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L^{i}_{t}-1}(\kappa^{i}_{\ell+1}-\kappa^{i}_{\ell})\left(\bar{b}^{i}_{t}-\bar{w}^{i}_{t\ell}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}^{i}}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L^{i}_{t}-1}(\kappa^{i}_{\ell+1}-\kappa^{i}_{\ell})\left({b}^{i}_{t}-{w}^{i}_{t\ell}\right)

\displaystyle=\bar{f}(b,w).

Indeed, the first equality follows from the definition of
\bar{f}(\cdot,\cdot)
. The inequality follows from line (
63
), from the facts that
\bar{b}^{i}_{T^{i}}\geq b^{i}_{T^{i}}
and
\bar{b}^{i}_{T}\geq b^{i}_{T}
, and from the fact that each quantity
\kappa^{i}_{\ell+1}-\kappa^{i}_{\ell}
is strictly positive. The final equality follows from the definition of
\bar{f}(\cdot,\cdot)
. Since
b,w
was an optimal solution for (
H
), our proof of Lemma
4.9
is complete.
\halmos

Proof H.3

Proof of Lemma
4.10
.
Let
f(b,w)=\bar{f}(b,w)
. In this case, we recall from Lemma
4.9
that there exists an optimal solution
b,w
for (
H
) that satisfies
b^{1}_{T}=\cdots=b^{N}_{T}=1
and
b^{i}_{t}=0
for each sample path
i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
and period
t\in\{1,\ldots,T\}\setminus\mathcal{T}^{i}
. Therefore, we can without loss of generality impose those equality constraints into the optimization problem (
H
). That is, (
H
) can be rewritten equivalently as

\displaystyle\underset{b,w}{\textnormal{maximize}}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}^{i}}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L^{i}_{t}-1}(\kappa^{i}_{\ell+1}-\kappa^{i}_{\ell})\left(b^{i}_{t}-w^{i}_{t\ell}\right)

(H-1)

\displaystyle\textnormal{subject to}

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &w^{i}_{t\ell}\leq w^{i}_{t+1,\ell}&&\textnormal{for all }i\in\{1,\ldots,N\},\;t\in\{1,\ldots,T-1\},\;\ell\in\{1,\ldots,|\mathcal{K}^{i}|\}\\
&w^{i}_{t\ell}\leq w^{i}_{t,\ell+1}&&\textnormal{for all }i\in\{1,\ldots,N\},\;t\in\{1,\ldots,T\},\;\ell\in\{1,\ldots,|\mathcal{K}^{i}|-1\}\\
&b^{i}_{t}\leq w^{i}_{t+1,1}&&\textnormal{for all }i\in\{1,\ldots,N\},\;t\in\{1,\ldots,T-1\}\\
&b^{j}_{t}\leq w^{i}_{t\ell}&&\textnormal{for all }i,j\in\{1,\ldots,N\}\textnormal{ and }t\in\{1,\dots,T\}\textnormal{ such that }g(t,x^{i})=\kappa^{i}_{\ell}\\
&&&\textnormal{and }\mathcal{U}^{i}_{t}\cap\mathcal{U}^{j}_{t}\neq\emptyset\\
&b^{i}_{T}=1&&\textnormal{for all }i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}\\
&b^{i}_{t}=0&&\textnormal{for all }i\in\{1,\ldots,N\},\;t\in\{1,\ldots,T\}\setminus\mathcal{T}^{i}\\
&b^{i}_{t}\in\{0,1\}&&\textnormal{for all }i\in\{1,\ldots,N\},\;t\in\{1,\dots,T\}\\
&w^{i}_{t\ell}\in\mathbb{R}&&\textnormal{for all }i\in\{1,\ldots,N\},\;t\in\{1,\ldots,T\},\;\ell\in\{1,\ldots,|\mathcal{K}^{i}|\}.\end{aligned}

After substituting out the decision variables
b^{i}_{t}
that have been set to zero or one, it follows from algebra that the above optimization problem can be rewritten equivalently as

\displaystyle\underset{b,w}{\textnormal{maximize}}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}:\;T^{i}<T}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L^{i}_{T^{i}}-1}(\kappa^{i}_{\ell+1}-\kappa^{i}_{\ell})\left(b^{i}_{T^{i}}-w^{i}_{T^{i}\ell}\right)+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L^{i}_{T}-1}(\kappa^{i}_{\ell+1}-\kappa^{i}_{\ell})\left(1-w^{i}_{T\ell}\right)

(H-2)

\displaystyle\textnormal{subject to}

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &w^{i}_{t\ell}\leq w^{i}_{t+1,\ell}&&\textnormal{for all }i\in\{1,\ldots,N\},\;t\in\{1,\ldots,T-1\},\;\ell\in\{1,\ldots,|\mathcal{K}^{i}|\}\\
&w^{i}_{t\ell}\leq w^{i}_{t,\ell+1}&&\textnormal{for all }i\in\{1,\ldots,N\},\;t\in\{1,\ldots,T\},\;\ell\in\{1,\ldots,|\mathcal{K}^{i}|-1\}\\
&b^{i}_{T^{i}}\leq w^{i}_{T^{i}+1,1}&&\textnormal{for all }i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}\textnormal{ such that }T^{i}<T\\
&b^{j}_{T^{j}}\leq w^{i}_{T^{j}\ell}&&\textnormal{for all }i,j\in\{1,\ldots,N\}\textnormal{ such that }T^{j}<T,\;g(T^{j},x^{i})=\kappa^{i}_{\ell},\;\textnormal{and }\mathcal{U}^{i}_{T^{j}}\cap\mathcal{U}^{j}_{T^{j}}\neq\emptyset\\
&w^{i}_{T,L^{i}_{T}}=1&&\textnormal{for all }i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}\\
&b^{i}_{T^{i}}\in\{0,1\}&&\textnormal{for all }i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}\textnormal{ such that }T^{i}<T\\
&w^{i}_{t\ell}\in\mathbb{R}&&\textnormal{for all }i\in\{1,\ldots,N\},\;t\in\{1,\ldots,T\},\;\ell\in\{1,\ldots,|\mathcal{K}^{i}|\}.\end{aligned}

Let us make three observations about the above optimization problem. First, we observe from inspection that the constraints in the above optimization problem of the form
w^{i}_{T,L^{i}_{T}}=1
for all
i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
can be removed from the above optimization problem without affecting its optimal objective value. Second, we observe that the decision variables
w^{i}_{t\ell}
for each
t\in\{1,\ldots,T\}\setminus\mathcal{T}^{i}
do not appear in the objective function of (
H-2
). Third, since each term
\kappa^{i}_{\ell+1}-\kappa^{i}_{\ell}
is strictly positive, we observe that there exists an optimal solution for (
H-2
) that satisfies the following equality for each sample path
i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
, period
t\in\mathcal{T}^{i}
, and
\ell\in\{1,\ldots,|\mathcal{K}^{i}|\}
:

\displaystyle w^{i}_{t\ell}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}0,&\text{if }\left[b^{j}_{T^{j}}=0\textnormal{ for all }j\in\{1,\ldots,N\}\textnormal{ such that }T^{j}\leq t,\;T^{j}<T,\;g(T^{j},x^{i})\leq\kappa^{i}_{\ell},\;\textnormal{and }\mathcal{U}^{i}_{T^{j}}\cap\mathcal{U}^{j}_{T^{j}}\neq\emptyset\right]\\
&\text{and }\left[\textnormal{if }T^{i}<t,\textnormal{ then }b^{i}_{T^{i}}=0\right],\\
1,&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

It follows from the aforementioned three observations that (
H-2
) can be rewritten equivalently as

\displaystyle\underset{b,w}{\textnormal{maximize}}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}:T^{i}<T}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L^{i}_{T^{i}}-1}(\kappa^{i}_{\ell+1}-\kappa^{i}_{\ell})\left(b^{i}_{T^{i}}-w^{i}_{T^{i}\ell}\right)+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L^{i}_{T}-1}(\kappa^{i}_{\ell+1}-\kappa^{i}_{\ell})\left(1-w^{i}_{T\ell}\right)

(

¯
H

)

\displaystyle\textnormal{subject to}

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &w^{i}_{t\ell}\leq w^{i}_{t,\ell+1}&&\textnormal{for all }i\in\{1,\ldots,N\},\;t\in\mathcal{T}^{i},\;\ell\in\{1,\ldots,|\mathcal{K}^{i}|-1\}\\
&b^{i}_{T^{i}}\leq w^{i}_{T1}&&\textnormal{for all }i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}\textnormal{ such that }T^{i}<T\\
&b^{j}_{T^{j}}\leq w^{i}_{t\ell}&&\textnormal{for all }i,j\in\{1,\ldots,N\}\textnormal{ and }t\in\mathcal{T}^{i}\textnormal{ such that }g(T^{j},x^{i})=\kappa^{i}_{\ell},\\
&&&T^{j}\leq t,\;T^{j}<T,\;\textnormal{and }\mathcal{U}^{i}_{T^{j}}\cap\mathcal{U}^{j}_{T^{j}}\neq\emptyset\\
&b^{i}_{T^{i}}\in\{0,1\}&&\textnormal{for all }i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}\textnormal{ such that }T^{i}<T\\
&w^{i}_{t\ell}\in\mathbb{R}&&\textnormal{for all }i\in\{1,\ldots,N\},\;t\in\mathcal{T}^{i},\;\ell\in\{1,\ldots,|\mathcal{K}^{i}|\}.\end{aligned}

We have thus shown that the optimal objective value of (

¯
H

) is equal to the optimal objective value of (
H
) when
f(b,w)=\bar{f}(b,w)
. Moreover, it follows from the above reasoning that any optimal solution
\bar{b},\bar{w}
for (

¯
H

) can be transformed into an optimal solution for (
H
) using the following equality:

\displaystyle{b}^{i}_{t}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\bar{b}^{i}_{t},&\textnormal{if }t=T^{i}\textnormal{ and }T^{i}<T,\\
1,&\textnormal{if }t=T,\\
0,&\textnormal{otherwise}.\end{cases}

This concludes our proof of Lemma
4.10
.

\halmos

Proof H.4

Proof of Proposition
4.8
.

We observe that (

¯
H

) is equivalent to a binary linear optimization problem with
\mathcal{O}(NT)
binary decision variables and
\mathcal{O}(N^{2}+NT)
constraints, with each constraint of the form
\lambda_{i}\geq\lambda_{j}
.
It thus follows from
Picard 1976
that the optimization problem (

¯
H

) is equivalent to a problem of computing the maximal closure of a directed graph with
\mathcal{O}(NT)
nodes and
\mathcal{O}(N^{2}+NT)
edges. Furthermore,
Picard 1976
shows that any maximal closure problem can be solved by computing the maximum flow in an augmented graph of identical size. Applying the algorithm of
Orlin 2013
to compute the maximum flow in this augmented graph, we obtain an
\mathcal{O}(N^{2}T(N+T))
algorithm for solving (

¯
H

). Finally, the output of the maximal closure problem can be transformed into an optimal solution for (
H
) using Lemma
4.10
. This concludes our proof of Proposition
4.8
.

\halmos

H.3
Proofs from §
4.2.4

Proof H.5

Proof of Proposition
4.11
.
Suppose that the number of periods is
T=2
. We recall from Remark
4.7
that the optimal objective value of (

¯
H

) is less than or equal to the optimal objective value of (
BP
). Therefore, it remains for us to show that the optimal objective value of (
BP
) is less than or equal to the optimal objective value of (

¯
H

).

We begin by restating the optimization problem (
BP
) when
T=2
for the sake of convenience:

\displaystyle\underset{b,w}{\textnormal{maximize}}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L^{i}_{1}-1}(\kappa^{i}_{\ell+1}-\kappa^{i}_{\ell})b^{i}_{1}(1-w^{i}_{1\ell})+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L^{i}_{2}-1}(\kappa^{i}_{\ell+1}-\kappa^{i}_{\ell})b^{i}_{2}(1-w^{i}_{2\ell})

(
BP
)

\displaystyle\textnormal{subject to}

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &w^{i}_{1\ell}\leq w^{i}_{2\ell}&&\textnormal{for all }i\in\{1,\ldots,N\},\;\ell\in\{1,\ldots,|\mathcal{K}^{i}|\}\\
&w^{i}_{t\ell}\leq w^{i}_{t,\ell+1}&&\textnormal{for all }i\in\{1,\ldots,N\},\;t\in\{1,2\},\;\ell\in\{1,\ldots,|\mathcal{K}^{i}|-1\}\\
&b^{i}_{1}\leq w^{i}_{21}&&\textnormal{for all }i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}\\
&b^{j}_{1}\leq w^{i}_{1\ell}&&\textnormal{for all }i,j\in\{1,\ldots,N\}\textnormal{ such that }g(1,x^{i})=\kappa^{i}_{\ell}\;\textnormal{and }\mathcal{U}^{i}_{1}\cap\mathcal{U}^{j}_{1}\neq\emptyset\\
&b^{j}_{2}\leq w^{i}_{2\ell}&&\textnormal{for all }i,j\in\{1,\ldots,N\}\textnormal{ such that }g(2,x^{i})=\kappa^{i}_{\ell}\;\textnormal{and }\mathcal{U}^{i}_{2}\cap\mathcal{U}^{j}_{2}\neq\emptyset\\
&b^{i}_{t}\in\{0,1\}&&\textnormal{for all }i\in\{1,\ldots,N\},\;t\in\{1,2\}\\
&w^{i}_{t\ell}\in\mathbb{R}&&\textnormal{for all }i\in\{1,\ldots,N\},\;t\in\{1,2\},\;\ell\in\{1,\ldots,|\mathcal{K}^{i}|\}.\end{aligned}

We readily observe from inspection of the optimization problem (
BP
) that there exists an optimal solution
b,w
for (
BP
) that satisfies the equalities
b^{1}_{2}=\cdots=b^{N}_{2}=1
and
b^{i}_{1}=0
for all
i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
such that
T^{i}=2
. Therefore, we observe that those equality constraints can be added to the optimization problem (
BP
) without changing its optimal objective value. That is, the optimization problem (
BP
) can be rewritten equivalently as

\displaystyle\underset{b,w}{\textnormal{maximize}}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L^{i}_{1}-1}(\kappa^{i}_{\ell+1}-\kappa^{i}_{\ell})b^{i}_{1}(1-w^{i}_{1\ell})+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L^{i}_{2}-1}(\kappa^{i}_{\ell+1}-\kappa^{i}_{\ell})b^{i}_{2}(1-w^{i}_{2\ell})

\displaystyle\textnormal{subject to}

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &w^{i}_{1\ell}\leq w^{i}_{2\ell}&&\textnormal{for all }i\in\{1,\ldots,N\},\;\ell\in\{1,\ldots,|\mathcal{K}^{i}|\}\\
&w^{i}_{t\ell}\leq w^{i}_{t,\ell+1}&&\textnormal{for all }i\in\{1,\ldots,N\},\;t\in\{1,2\},\;\ell\in\{1,\ldots,|\mathcal{K}^{i}|-1\}\\
&b^{i}_{1}\leq w^{i}_{21}&&\textnormal{for all }i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}\\
&b^{j}_{1}\leq w^{i}_{1\ell}&&\textnormal{for all }i,j\in\{1,\ldots,N\}\textnormal{ such that }g(1,x^{i})=\kappa^{i}_{\ell}\;\textnormal{and }\mathcal{U}^{i}_{1}\cap\mathcal{U}^{j}_{1}\neq\emptyset\\
&b^{j}_{2}\leq w^{i}_{2\ell}&&\textnormal{for all }i,j\in\{1,\ldots,N\}\textnormal{ such that }g(2,x^{i})=\kappa^{i}_{\ell}\;\textnormal{and }\mathcal{U}^{i}_{2}\cap\mathcal{U}^{j}_{2}\neq\emptyset\\
&b^{i}_{2}=1&&\textnormal{for all }i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}\\
&b^{i}_{1}=0&&\textnormal{for all }i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}\textnormal{ such that }T^{i}=2\\
&b^{i}_{t}\in\{0,1\}&&\textnormal{for all }i\in\{1,\ldots,N\},\;t\in\{1,2\}\\
&w^{i}_{t\ell}\in\mathbb{R}&&\textnormal{for all }i\in\{1,\ldots,N\},\;t\in\{1,2\},\;\ell\in\{1,\ldots,|\mathcal{K}^{i}|\}.\end{aligned}

After eliminating the decision variables
b^{i}_{t}
that have been constrained to be equal to zero or one, the above optimization problem can be rewritten equivalently as

\displaystyle\underset{b,w}{\textnormal{maximize}}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}:T^{i}=1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L^{i}_{1}-1}(\kappa^{i}_{\ell+1}-\kappa^{i}_{\ell})b^{i}_{1}(1-w^{i}_{1\ell})+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L^{i}_{2}-1}(\kappa^{i}_{\ell+1}-\kappa^{i}_{\ell})(1-w^{i}_{2\ell})

\displaystyle\textnormal{subject to}

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &w^{i}_{1\ell}\leq w^{i}_{2\ell}&&\textnormal{for all }i\in\{1,\ldots,N\},\ell\in\{1,\ldots,|\mathcal{K}^{i}|\}\\
&w^{i}_{t\ell}\leq w^{i}_{t,\ell+1}&&\textnormal{for all }i\in\{1,\ldots,N\},\;t\in\{1,2\},\;\ell\in\{1,\ldots,|\mathcal{K}^{i}|-1\}\\
&b^{i}_{1}\leq w^{i}_{21}&&\textnormal{for all }i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}\textnormal{ such that }T^{i}=1\\
&b^{j}_{1}\leq w^{i}_{1\ell}&&\textnormal{for all }i,j\in\{1,\ldots,N\}\textnormal{ such that }g(1,x^{i})=\kappa^{i}_{\ell},\;T^{j}=1,\;\textnormal{and }\mathcal{U}^{i}_{1}\cap\mathcal{U}^{j}_{1}\neq\emptyset\\
&w^{i}_{2,L^{i}_{2}}=1&&\textnormal{for all }i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}\\
&b^{i}_{1}\in\{0,1\}&&\textnormal{for all }i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}\textnormal{ such that }T^{i}=1\\
&w^{i}_{t\ell}\in\mathbb{R}&&\textnormal{for all }i\in\{1,\ldots,N\},\;t\in\{1,2\},\;\ell\in\{1,\ldots,|\mathcal{K}^{i}|\}.\end{aligned}

Let us make two observations about the above optimization problem. First, we observe from inspection that the constraints in the above optimization problem of the form
w^{i}_{2,L^{i}_{2}}=1
for all
i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
can be removed from the above optimization problem without affecting its optimal objective value. Second, since each term
\kappa^{i}_{\ell+1}-\kappa^{i}_{\ell}
is strictly positive, we observe that there exists an optimal solution for the above optimization problem in which the equalities
w^{i}_{11}=\cdots=w^{i}_{1,L^{i}_{1}-1}=0
are satisfied for each sample path
i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
. Therefore, the above optimization problem can be rewritten equivalently as

\displaystyle\underset{b,w}{\textnormal{maximize}}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}:T^{i}=1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L^{i}_{1}-1}(\kappa^{i}_{\ell+1}-\kappa^{i}_{\ell})b^{i}_{1}(1-w^{i}_{1\ell})+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L^{i}_{2}-1}(\kappa^{i}_{\ell+1}-\kappa^{i}_{\ell})(1-w^{i}_{2\ell})

\displaystyle\textnormal{subject to}

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &w^{i}_{1\ell}\leq w^{i}_{2\ell}&&\textnormal{for all }i\in\{1,\ldots,N\},\ell\in\{1,\ldots,|\mathcal{K}^{i}|\}\\
&w^{i}_{t\ell}\leq w^{i}_{t,\ell+1}&&\textnormal{for all }i\in\{1,\ldots,N\},\;t\in\{1,2\},\;\ell\in\{1,\ldots,|\mathcal{K}^{i}|-1\}\\
&b^{i}_{1}\leq w^{i}_{21}&&\textnormal{for all }i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}\textnormal{ such that }T^{i}=1\\
&b^{j}_{1}\leq w^{i}_{1\ell}&&\textnormal{for all }i,j\in\{1,\ldots,N\}\textnormal{ such that }g(1,x^{i})=\kappa^{i}_{\ell},\;T^{j}=1,\;\textnormal{and }\mathcal{U}^{i}_{1}\cap\mathcal{U}^{j}_{1}\neq\emptyset\\
&w^{i}_{1\ell}=0&&\textnormal{for all }i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}\textnormal{ and }\ell\in\{1,\ldots,L^{i}_{1}-1\}\\
&b^{i}_{1}\in\{0,1\}&&\textnormal{for all }i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}\textnormal{ such that }T^{i}=1\\
&w^{i}_{t\ell}\in\mathbb{R}&&\textnormal{for all }i\in\{1,\ldots,N\},\;t\in\{1,2\},\;\ell\in\{1,\ldots,|\mathcal{K}^{i}|\}.\end{aligned}

Because the above optimization problem has the constraint that
w^{i}_{1\ell}=0
for all
i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
and
\ell\in\{1,\ldots,L^{i}_{1}-1\}
, we can without loss of generality replace each term
b^{i}_{1}(1-w^{i}_{1\ell})
in the objective function of the above optimization problem with
b^{i}_{1}-w^{i}_{1\ell}
. That is, the above optimization problem is equivalent to

\displaystyle\underset{b,w}{\textnormal{maximize}}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}:T^{i}=1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L^{i}_{1}-1}(\kappa^{i}_{\ell+1}-\kappa^{i}_{\ell})(b^{i}_{1}-w^{i}_{1\ell})+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L^{i}_{2}-1}(\kappa^{i}_{\ell+1}-\kappa^{i}_{\ell})(1-w^{i}_{2\ell})

\displaystyle\textnormal{subject to}

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &w^{i}_{1\ell}\leq w^{i}_{2\ell}&&\textnormal{for all }i\in\{1,\ldots,N\},\ell\in\{1,\ldots,|\mathcal{K}^{i}|\}\\
&w^{i}_{t\ell}\leq w^{i}_{t,\ell+1}&&\textnormal{for all }i\in\{1,\ldots,N\},\;t\in\{1,2\},\;\ell\in\{1,\ldots,|\mathcal{K}^{i}|-1\}\\
&b^{i}_{1}\leq w^{i}_{21}&&\textnormal{for all }i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}\textnormal{ such that }T^{i}=1\\
&b^{j}_{1}\leq w^{i}_{1\ell}&&\textnormal{for all }i,j\in\{1,\ldots,N\}\textnormal{ such that }g(1,x^{i})=\kappa^{i}_{\ell},\;T^{j}=1,\;\textnormal{and }\mathcal{U}^{i}_{1}\cap\mathcal{U}^{j}_{1}\neq\emptyset\\
&w^{i}_{1\ell}=0&&\textnormal{for all }i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}\textnormal{ and }\ell\in\{1,\ldots,L^{i}_{1}-1\}\\
&b^{i}_{1}\in\{0,1\}&&\textnormal{for all }i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}\textnormal{ such that }T^{i}=1\\
&w^{i}_{t\ell}\in\mathbb{R}&&\textnormal{for all }i\in\{1,\ldots,N\},\;t\in\{1,2\},\;\ell\in\{1,\ldots,|\mathcal{K}^{i}|\}.\end{aligned}

Finally, we can relax the above optimization problem by removing the constraint that
w^{i}_{1\ell}=0
for all
i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
and
\ell\in\{1,\ldots,L^{i}_{1}-1\}
. That is, the optimal objective value of the above optimization problem is less than or equal to the optimal objective value of the following optimization problem:

\displaystyle\underset{b,w}{\textnormal{maximize}}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}:T^{i}=1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L^{i}_{1}-1}(\kappa^{i}_{\ell+1}-\kappa^{i}_{\ell})(b^{i}_{1}-w^{i}_{1\ell})+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L^{i}_{2}-1}(\kappa^{i}_{\ell+1}-\kappa^{i}_{\ell})(1-w^{i}_{2\ell})

\displaystyle\textnormal{subject to}

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &w^{i}_{1\ell}\leq w^{i}_{2\ell}&&\textnormal{for all }i\in\{1,\ldots,N\},\ell\in\{1,\ldots,|\mathcal{K}^{i}|\}\\
&w^{i}_{t\ell}\leq w^{i}_{t,\ell+1}&&\textnormal{for all }i\in\{1,\ldots,N\},\;t\in\{1,2\},\;\ell\in\{1,\ldots,|\mathcal{K}^{i}|-1\}\\
&b^{i}_{1}\leq w^{i}_{21}&&\textnormal{for all }i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}\textnormal{ such that }T^{i}=1\\
&b^{j}_{1}\leq w^{i}_{1\ell}&&\textnormal{for all }i,j\in\{1,\ldots,N\}\textnormal{ such that }g(1,x^{i})=\kappa^{i}_{\ell},\;T^{j}=1,\;\textnormal{and }\mathcal{U}^{i}_{1}\cap\mathcal{U}^{j}_{1}\neq\emptyset\\
&b^{i}_{1}\in\{0,1\}&&\textnormal{for all }i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}\textnormal{ such that }T^{i}=1\\
&w^{i}_{t\ell}\in\mathbb{R}&&\textnormal{for all }i\in\{1,\ldots,N\},\;t\in\{1,2\},\;\ell\in\{1,\ldots,|\mathcal{K}^{i}|\}.\end{aligned}

We observe that the above optimization problem is equivalent to (

¯
H

) for the case where
T=2
. Therefore, we have shown that the optimal objective value of (
BP
) is less than or equal to the optimal objective value of (

¯
H

), which concludes our proof of Proposition
4.11
.

\halmos

Our proofs of Propositions
4.12
and
4.13
will make use of two intermediary results, denoted below by Lemmas
H.6
and
H.8
. These intermediary lemmas establish an upper bound on the optimal objective value of (
BP
) and a lower bound on the optimal objective value of (

¯
H

), respectively. Throughout the proofs, we let
J^{\textnormal{\ref{prob:bp}}}
denote the optimal objective value of (
BP
) and
J^{\textnormal{\ref{prob:h_bar}}}
denote the optimal objective value of (

¯
H

).

Lemma H.6

J^{\textnormal{\ref{prob:bp}}}\leq\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}g(T^{i},x^{i})
.

Proof H.7

Proof of Lemma
H.6
.
By removing constraints from the optimization problem (
BP
), we obtain the following optimization problem:

\displaystyle\underset{b,w}{\textnormal{maximize}}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L^{i}_{t}-1}(\kappa^{i}_{\ell+1}-\kappa^{i}_{\ell})b^{i}_{t}(1-w^{i}_{t\ell})

\displaystyle\textnormal{subject to}

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &w^{i}_{t\ell}\leq w^{i}_{t+1,\ell}&&\textnormal{for all }i\in\{1,\ldots,N\},\;t\in\{1,\ldots,T-1\},\;\ell\in\{1,\ldots,|\mathcal{K}^{i}|\}\\
&w^{i}_{t\ell}\leq w^{i}_{t,\ell+1}&&\textnormal{for all }i\in\{1,\ldots,N\},\;t\in\{1,\ldots,T\},\;\ell\in\{1,\ldots,|\mathcal{K}^{i}|-1\}\\
&b^{i}_{t}\leq w^{i}_{t+1,1}&&\textnormal{for all }i\in\{1,\ldots,N\},\;t\in\{1,\ldots,T-1\}\\
&b^{i}_{t}\in\{0,1\}&&\textnormal{for all }i\in\{1,\ldots,N\},\;t\in\{1,\dots,T\}\\
&w^{i}_{t\ell}\in\mathbb{R}&&\textnormal{for all }i\in\{1,\ldots,N\},\;t\in\{1,\ldots,T\},\;\ell\in\{1,\ldots,|\mathcal{K}^{i}|\}.\end{aligned}

In greater detail, we observe that the above optimization problem is identical to (
BP
), with the exception that the above optimization problem does not include the constraints of the form
b^{j}_{t}\leq w^{i}_{t\ell}
for all
i,j\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
and
t\in\{1,\dots,T\}
such that
g(t,x^{i})=\kappa^{i}_{\ell}
and
\mathcal{U}^{i}_{t}\cap\mathcal{U}^{j}_{t}\neq\emptyset
. Consequently, the optimal objective value of the above optimization problem is greater than or equal to the optimal objective value of (
BP
). Moreover, it follows from inspection and from the fact that each term
\kappa^{i}_{\ell+1}-\kappa^{i}_{\ell}
is strictly positive that there exists an optimal solution for the above optimization problem that satisfies
b^{i}_{t}=\mathbb{I}\left\{t=T^{i}\right\}
for each sample path
i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
. Therefore, the optimal objective value of the above optimization problem is equal to

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L^{i}_{t}-1}(\kappa^{i}_{\ell+1}-\kappa^{i}_{\ell})\mathbb{I}\left\{t=T^{i}\right\}(1-w^{i}_{t\ell})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L^{i}_{T^{i}}-1}(\kappa^{i}_{\ell+1}-\kappa^{i}_{\ell})(1-w^{i}_{T^{i}\ell})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L^{i}_{T^{i}}-1}(\kappa^{i}_{\ell+1}-\kappa^{i}_{\ell})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}g(T^{i},x^{i}),

where the first equality follows from algebra, the inequality follows form the fact that each decision variable
w^{i}_{T^{i}\ell}
must be greater than or equal to zero, and the final equality follows from the definitions of the constants
\kappa^{i}_{\ell}
and
L^{i}_{t}
. This concludes our proof of Lemma
H.6
.

\halmos

Lemma H.8

J^{\textnormal{\ref{prob:h_bar}}}
is greater than or equal to the optimal objective value of the following optimization problem:

\displaystyle\underset{b,w}{\textnormal{maximize}}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L^{i}_{T^{i}}-1}(\kappa^{i}_{\ell+1}-\kappa^{i}_{\ell})\left(b^{i}_{T^{i}}-w^{i}_{T^{i}\ell}\right)

(
\bar{\textnormal{H}}
-LB)

\displaystyle\textnormal{subject to}

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &w^{i}_{T^{i}\ell}\leq w^{i}_{T^{i},\ell+1}&&\textnormal{for all }i\in\{1,\ldots,N\},\;\ell\in\{1,\ldots,|\mathcal{K}^{i}|-1\}\\
&b^{j}_{T^{j}}\leq w^{i}_{T^{i}\ell}&&\textnormal{for all }i,j\in\{1,\ldots,N\}\textnormal{ such that }g(T^{j},x^{i})=\kappa^{i}_{\ell},\\
&&&\kappa^{i}_{\ell}<g(T^{i},x^{i}),\;T^{j}<T^{i},\;\textnormal{and }\mathcal{U}^{i}_{T^{j}}\cap\mathcal{U}^{j}_{T^{j}}\neq\emptyset\\
&b^{i}_{T^{i}}\in\{0,1\}&&\textnormal{for all }i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}\\
&w^{i}_{T^{i}\ell}\in\mathbb{R}&&\textnormal{for all }i\in\{1,\ldots,N\},\;\ell\in\{1,\ldots,|\mathcal{K}^{i}|-1\}.\end{aligned}

Proof H.9

Proof of Lemma
H.8
.
We begin by rewriting (

¯
H

) as the following equivalent optimization problem:

\displaystyle\underset{b,w}{\textnormal{maximize}}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L^{i}_{T^{i}}-1}(\kappa^{i}_{\ell+1}-\kappa^{i}_{\ell})\left(b^{i}_{T^{i}}-w^{i}_{T^{i}\ell}\right)+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}:T^{i}<T}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L^{i}_{T}-1}(\kappa^{i}_{\ell+1}-\kappa^{i}_{\ell})\left(1-w^{i}_{T\ell}\right)

(
\bar{\textnormal{H}}^{\prime}
)

\displaystyle\textnormal{subject to}

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &w^{i}_{t\ell}\leq w^{i}_{t,\ell+1}&&\textnormal{for all }i\in\{1,\ldots,N\},\;t\in\mathcal{T}^{i},\;\ell\in\{1,\ldots,|\mathcal{K}^{i}|-1\}\\
&b^{i}_{T^{i}}\leq w^{i}_{T1}&&\textnormal{for all }i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}\textnormal{ such that }T^{i}<T\\
&b^{j}_{T^{j}}\leq w^{i}_{t\ell}&&\textnormal{for all }i,j\in\{1,\ldots,N\}\textnormal{ and }t\in\mathcal{T}^{i}\textnormal{ such that }g(T^{j},x^{i})=\kappa^{i}_{\ell},\\
&&&T^{j}\leq t,\;T^{j}<T,\;\textnormal{and }\mathcal{U}^{i}_{T^{j}}\cap\mathcal{U}^{j}_{T^{j}}\neq\emptyset\\
&b^{i}_{T^{i}}\in\{0,1\}&&\textnormal{for all }i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}\\
&w^{i}_{t\ell}\in\mathbb{R}&&\textnormal{for all }i\in\{1,\ldots,N\},\;t\in\mathcal{T}^{i},\;\ell\in\{1,\ldots,|\mathcal{K}^{i}|\}.\end{aligned}

We claim that the optimal objective value of the above optimization problem (

¯
H

′

) is equal to the optimal objective value of (

¯
H

). To see why this is true, we first observe that the above optimization problem is identical to (

¯
H

), except for the fact that the term
\left(1-w^{i}_{T\ell}\right)
in the objective function of (

¯
H

) has been replaced with
\left(b^{i}_{T}-w^{i}_{T\ell}\right)
in the objective function of (

¯
H

′

) for each
i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
such that
T^{i}=T
. Because the new decision variables
b^{i}_{T^{i}}\in\{0,1\}
for each
i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
such that
T^{i}=T
do not appear in any inequality constraints, and because each term
\kappa^{i}_{\ell+1}-\kappa^{i}_{\ell}
is strictly positive, we observe that there exists an optimal solution for the above optimization problem in which
b^{i}_{T^{i}}=1
for each
i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
such that
T^{i}=T
. Hence, we conclude that the optimal objective value of (

¯
H

′

) is equal to the optimal objective value of (

¯
H

).

We now construct a lower bound approximation of (

¯
H

′

) by modifying its objective function and adding constraints. Indeed, we first observe for every feasible solution for (

¯
H

′

) that the objective function of (

¯
H

′

) satisfies

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L^{i}_{T^{i}}-1}(\kappa^{i}_{\ell+1}-\kappa^{i}_{\ell})\left(b^{i}_{T^{i}}-w^{i}_{T^{i}\ell}\right)+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}:T^{i}<T}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L^{i}_{T}-1}(\kappa^{i}_{\ell+1}-\kappa^{i}_{\ell})\left(1-w^{i}_{T\ell}\right)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L^{i}_{T^{i}}-1}(\kappa^{i}_{\ell+1}-\kappa^{i}_{\ell})\left(b^{i}_{T^{i}}-w^{i}_{T^{i}\ell}\right),

where the inequality holds because each term
\kappa^{i}_{\ell+1}-\kappa^{i}_{\ell}
is strictly positive and because the decision variables
w^{i}_{T\ell}
are always nonnegative. Therefore, a lower bound on the optimal objective value of (

¯
H

′

) is given by the optimal objective value of the following optimization problem:

\displaystyle\underset{b,w}{\textnormal{maximize}}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L^{i}_{T^{i}}-1}(\kappa^{i}_{\ell+1}-\kappa^{i}_{\ell})\left(b^{i}_{T^{i}}-w^{i}_{T^{i}\ell}\right)

(
\bar{\textnormal{H}}
-LB-1)

\displaystyle\textnormal{subject to}

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &w^{i}_{t\ell}\leq w^{i}_{t,\ell+1}&&\textnormal{for all }i\in\{1,\ldots,N\},\;t\in\mathcal{T}^{i},\;\ell\in\{1,\ldots,|\mathcal{K}^{i}|-1\}\\
&b^{i}_{T^{i}}\leq w^{i}_{T1}&&\textnormal{for all }i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}\textnormal{ such that }T^{i}<T\\
&b^{j}_{T^{j}}\leq w^{i}_{t\ell}&&\textnormal{for all }i,j\in\{1,\ldots,N\}\textnormal{ and }t\in\mathcal{T}^{i}\textnormal{ such that }g(T^{j},x^{i})=\kappa^{i}_{\ell},\\
&&&T^{j}\leq t,\;T^{j}<T,\;\textnormal{and }\mathcal{U}^{i}_{T^{j}}\cap\mathcal{U}^{j}_{T^{j}}\neq\emptyset\\
&b^{i}_{T^{i}}\in\{0,1\}&&\textnormal{for all }i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}\\
&w^{i}_{t\ell}\in\mathbb{R}&&\textnormal{for all }i\in\{1,\ldots,N\},\;t\in\mathcal{T}^{i},\;\ell\in\{1,\ldots,|\mathcal{K}^{i}|\}.\end{aligned}

Moreover, we observe from inspection that the optimal objective value of the above optimization problem would not change if we added the constraints
w^{i}_{T\ell}=1
to the above optimization problem for each sample path
i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
and
\ell\in\{1,\ldots,|\mathcal{K}^{i}|\}
that satisfies
T^{i}<T
.
By eliminating these decision variables
w^{i}_{T\ell}
that can be constrained without loss of generality to be equal to one, it follows from algebra that the optimal objective value of the optimization problem (

¯
H

-LB-1
) is equal to

\displaystyle\underset{b,w}{\textnormal{maximize}}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L^{i}_{T^{i}}-1}(\kappa^{i}_{\ell+1}-\kappa^{i}_{\ell})\left(b^{i}_{T^{i}}-w^{i}_{T^{i}\ell}\right)

(
\bar{\textnormal{H}}
-LB-2)

\displaystyle\textnormal{subject to}

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &w^{i}_{T^{i}\ell}\leq w^{i}_{T^{i},\ell+1}&&\textnormal{for all }i\in\{1,\ldots,N\},\;\ell\in\{1,\ldots,|\mathcal{K}^{i}|-1\}\\
&b^{j}_{T^{j}}\leq w^{i}_{T^{i}\ell}&&\textnormal{for all }i,j\in\{1,\ldots,N\}\textnormal{ such that }g(T^{j},x^{i})=\kappa^{i}_{\ell},\\
&&&T^{j}\leq T^{i},\;T^{j}<T,\;\textnormal{and }\mathcal{U}^{i}_{T^{j}}\cap\mathcal{U}^{j}_{T^{j}}\neq\emptyset\\
&b^{i}_{T^{i}}\in\{0,1\}&&\textnormal{for all }i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}\\
&w^{i}_{T^{i}\ell}\in\mathbb{R}&&\textnormal{for all }i\in\{1,\ldots,N\},\;\ell\in\{1,\ldots,|\mathcal{K}^{i}|\}.\end{aligned}

We further observe from inspection that the optimal objective value of the above optimization problem would not change if we added the constraints
w^{i}_{T^{i},|\mathcal{K}^{i}|}=1
to the above optimization problem for each sample path
i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
.
14
14

14

We note that the equality
L^{i}_{T^{i}}=|\mathcal{K}^{i}|
holds for each sample path
i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
. This equality can be verified by simply applying the definitions of
L^{i}_{t}
,
\mathcal{K}^{i}
, and
T^{i}
.
By eliminating these decision variables
w^{i}_{T^{i},|\mathcal{K}^{i}|}
that can be constrained without loss of generality to be equal to one, and by observing that the above optimization problem has a constraint of the form
b^{j}_{t}\leq w^{i}_{t\ell}
if and only if
\ell=|\mathcal{K}^{i}|
and
t=T^{i}=T^{j}
, we conclude that the optimal objective value of the optimization problem (

¯
H

-LB-2
) is equal to

\displaystyle\underset{b,w}{\textnormal{maximize}}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L^{i}_{T^{i}}-1}(\kappa^{i}_{\ell+1}-\kappa^{i}_{\ell})\left(b^{i}_{T^{i}}-w^{i}_{T^{i}\ell}\right)

(

¯
H

-LB
)

\displaystyle\textnormal{subject to}

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &w^{i}_{T^{i}\ell}\leq w^{i}_{T^{i},\ell+1}&&\textnormal{for all }i\in\{1,\ldots,N\},\;\ell\in\{1,\ldots,|\mathcal{K}^{i}|-1\}\\
&b^{j}_{T^{j}}\leq w^{i}_{T^{i}\ell}&&\textnormal{for all }i,j\in\{1,\ldots,N\}\textnormal{ such that }g(T^{j},x^{i})=\kappa^{i}_{\ell},\\
&&&\kappa^{i}_{\ell}<g(T^{i},x^{i}),\;T^{j}<T^{i},\;\textnormal{and }\mathcal{U}^{i}_{T^{j}}\cap\mathcal{U}^{j}_{T^{j}}\neq\emptyset\\
&b^{i}_{T^{i}}\in\{0,1\}&&\textnormal{for all }i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}\\
&w^{i}_{T^{i}\ell}\in\mathbb{R}&&\textnormal{for all }i\in\{1,\ldots,N\},\;\ell\in\{1,\ldots,|\mathcal{K}^{i}|-1\}.\end{aligned}

This concludes our proof of Lemma
H.8
.

\halmos

In view of the above Lemmas
H.6
and
H.8
, we now present the proofs of Propositions

and
4.13
.

Proof H.10

Proof of Proposition
4.12
.
In the above Lemma
H.6
, we showed that
J^{\textnormal{\ref{prob:bp}}}\leq\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}g(T^{i},x^{i})
. Therefore, it remains for us to show that
J^{\textnormal{\ref{prob:h_bar}}}\geq\frac{1}{T}(\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}g(T^{i},x^{i}))
. Indeed,
let

t^{*}\triangleq\argmax_{t\in\{1,\ldots,T\}}\sum_{i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}:T^{i}=t}g(T^{i},x^{i})

be defined as any period that maximizes the sum of the rewards
g(T^{i},x^{i})
over all of the sample paths
i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
that satisfy
T^{i}=t^{*}
. We observe from algebra and from our construction of
t^{*}
that the following inequality must hold:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}:T^{i}=t^{*}}g(T^{i},x^{i})\geq\frac{1}{T}\left(\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}g(T^{i},x^{i})\right).

(64)

Moreover, we recall from the above Lemma
H.8
that
J^{\textnormal{\ref{prob:h_bar}}}
is greater than or equal to the optimal objective value of the optimization problem (

¯
H

-LB
). Consider the solution
b,w
for the optimization problem (

¯
H

-LB
) that satisfies the following equalities for each sample path
i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
, period
t\in\{1,\ldots,T\}
, and
\ell\in\{1,\ldots,|\mathcal{K}^{i}|-1\}
:

\displaystyle b^{i}_{T^{i}}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}1,&\text{if }T^{i}\in\{t^{*},\ldots,T\},\\
0,&\text{if }T^{i}\in\{1,\ldots,t^{*}-1\};\end{cases}

\displaystyle w^{i}_{T^{i}\ell}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}1,&\text{if }T^{i}\in\{t^{*}+1,\ldots,T\},\\
0,&\text{if }T^{i}\in\{1,\ldots,t^{*}\}.\end{cases}

We observe from inspection that the solution
b,w
defined by the above equalities is a feasible solution for the optimization problem (

¯
H

-LB
). Therefore, the optimal objective value of (

¯
H

-LB
) is greater than or equal to

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L^{i}_{T^{i}}-1}(\kappa^{i}_{\ell+1}-\kappa^{i}_{\ell})\left(\mathbb{I}\left\{T^{i}\in\{t^{*},\ldots,T\}\right\}-\mathbb{I}\left\{T^{i}\in\{t^{*}+1,\ldots,T\}\right\}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L^{i}_{T^{i}}-1}(\kappa^{i}_{\ell+1}-\kappa^{i}_{\ell})\mathbb{I}\left\{T^{i}=t^{*}\right\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}:T^{i}=t^{*}}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L^{i}_{T^{i}}-1}(\kappa^{i}_{\ell+1}-\kappa^{i}_{\ell})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}:T^{i}=t^{*}}g(T^{i},x^{i}),

where the last equality follows from the definition of the constants
\kappa^{i}_{\ell}
. Combining the above equalities with line (
64
), our proof of Proposition
4.12
is complete.

\halmos

Proof H.11

Proof of Proposition
4.13
.
Let Assumption
4.2.4
hold. Our proof of Proposition
4.13
is split into the following two intermediary claims.

Claim 12

Assume without loss of generality that
g(T^{1},x^{1})\geq\cdots\geq g(T^{N},x^{N})
. Then,

\displaystyle J^{\textnormal{\ref{prob:h_bar}}}\geq\max_{i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}}\frac{i}{N}g(T^{i},x^{i})-2\epsilon L,

where
\epsilon
is the radius of the uncertainty sets (see §
2.2
) and
L
is the constant from Assumption
4.2.4
.

Proof H.12

Proof of Claim
12
.
Assume without loss of generality that
g(T^{1},x^{1})\geq\cdots\geq g(T^{N},x^{N})
, and consider any arbitrary
i^{*}\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
. In the remainder of the proof, we will show that

\displaystyle J^{\textnormal{\ref{prob:h_bar}}}\geq\frac{i^{*}}{N}g(T^{i^{*}},x^{i^{*}})-2\epsilon L.

To begin, we recall from the above Lemma
H.8
that
J^{\textnormal{\ref{prob:h_bar}}}
is greater than or equal to the optimal objective value of the optimization problem (

¯
H

-LB
). Next, consider the solution
b,w
for the optimization problem (

¯
H

-LB
) that satisfies the following equalities for each sample path
i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
, period
t\in\{1,\ldots,T\}
, and
\ell\in\{1,\ldots,|\mathcal{K}^{i}|-1\}
:

\displaystyle b^{i}_{T^{i}}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}1,&\text{if }i\in\{1,\ldots,i^{*}\},\\
0,&\text{if }i\in\{i^{*}+1,\ldots,N\};\end{cases}

\displaystyle w^{i}_{T^{i}\ell}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}1,&\text{if }\kappa^{i}_{\ell}\geq g(T^{i^{*}},x^{i^{*}})-2L\epsilon,\\
0,&\text{if }T^{i}\in\{1,\ldots,t^{*}\}.\end{cases}

We will now prove that the solution
b,w
defined by the above equalities is a feasible solution for the optimization problem (

¯
H

-LB
). Indeed, we readily observe from inspection that the solution
b,w
defined by the above equalities satisfies the first set of constraints in the optimization problem (

¯
H

-LB
), i.e.,

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &w^{i}_{T^{i}\ell}\leq w^{i}_{T^{i},\ell+1}&&\textnormal{for all }i\in\{1,\ldots,N\},\;\ell\in\{1,\ldots,|\mathcal{K}^{i}|-1\}.\end{aligned}

To show that the solution
b,w
satisfies the second set of constraints in the optimization problem (

¯
H

-LB
), consider any sample paths
i,j\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
such that
g(T^{j},x^{i})=\kappa^{i}_{\ell}
,
\kappa^{i}_{\ell}<g(T^{i},x^{i})
,
T^{j}<T^{i}
, and
\mathcal{U}^{i}_{T^{j}}\cap\mathcal{U}^{j}_{T^{j}}\neq\emptyset
. We have two cases to consider. First, suppose that the sample path
j
satisfies
j\in\{i^{*}+1,\ldots,N\}
. In this case, it follows from the fact that
b^{j}_{T^{j}}=0
that the inequality
b^{j}_{T^{j}}\leq w^{i}_{T^{i}\ell}
is satisfied. Second, suppose that sample path
j
satisfies
j\in\{1,\ldots,i^{*}\}
. In this case, we observe that

\displaystyle g(T^{j},x^{i})

\displaystyle=\kappa^{i}_{\ell}\geq g(T^{j},x^{j})-2\epsilon L\geq g(T^{i^{*}},x^{i^{*}})-2\epsilon L,

where the first inequality follows from Assumption
4.2.4
and from the fact that
\mathcal{U}^{i}_{T^{j}}\cap\mathcal{U}^{j}_{T^{j}}\neq\emptyset
, and the second inequality follows from the fact that
j\in\{1,\ldots,i^{*}\}
. Therefore, we conclude that from our construction of the solution
b,w
that
w^{i}_{T^{i}\ell}=1
, which implies that the inequality
b^{j}_{T^{j}}\leq w^{i}_{T^{i}\ell}
is satisfied. We have thus shown that the solution
b,w
satisfies the second set of constraints in the optimization problem (

¯
H

-LB
), i.e.,

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &b^{j}_{T^{j}}\leq w^{i}_{T^{i}\ell}&&\textnormal{for all }i,j\in\{1,\ldots,N\}\textnormal{ such that }g(T^{j},x^{i})=\kappa^{i}_{\ell},\\
&&&\kappa^{i}_{\ell}<g(T^{i},x^{i}),\;T^{j}<T^{i},\;\textnormal{and }\mathcal{U}^{i}_{T^{j}}\cap\mathcal{U}^{j}_{T^{j}}\neq\emptyset.\end{aligned}

This concludes our proof that the solution
b,w
is a feasible solution for the optimization problem (

¯
H

-LB
).

Since
b,w
is a feasible solution for the optimization problem (

¯
H

-LB
), we have the following:

\displaystyle J^{\textnormal{\ref{prob:h_bar}}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L^{i}_{T^{i}}-1}(\kappa^{i}_{\ell+1}-\kappa^{i}_{\ell})\left(\mathbb{I}\left\{i\in\{1,\ldots,i^{*}\}\right\}-\mathbb{I}\left\{\kappa^{i}_{\ell}\geq g(T^{i^{*}},x^{i^{*}})-2L\epsilon\right\}\right)

(65)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{i^{*}}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L^{i}_{T^{i}}-1}(\kappa^{i}_{\ell+1}-\kappa^{i}_{\ell})-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L^{i}_{T^{i}}-1}\mathbb{I}\left\{\kappa^{i}_{\ell}\geq g(T^{i^{*}},x^{i^{*}})-2L\epsilon\right\}(\kappa^{i}_{\ell+1}-\kappa^{i}_{\ell})

(66)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{i^{*}}g(T^{i},x^{i})-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{\ell=1}^{L^{i}_{T^{i}}-1}\mathbb{I}\left\{\kappa^{i}_{\ell}\geq g(T^{i^{*}},x^{i^{*}})-2L\epsilon\right\}(\kappa^{i}_{\ell+1}-\kappa^{i}_{\ell})

(67)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{i^{*}}g(T^{i},x^{i})

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{I}\left\{\exists\ell\in\{1,\ldots,L^{i}_{T^{i}}-1\}\textnormal{ such that }\kappa^{i}_{\ell}\geq g(T^{i^{*}},x^{i^{*}})-2L\epsilon\right\}\left(g(T^{i},x^{i})-\left(g(T^{i^{*}},x^{i^{*}})-2L\epsilon\right)\right)

(68)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{i^{*}}g(T^{i},x^{i})-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{I}\left\{g(T^{i},x^{i})\geq g(T^{i^{*}},x^{i^{*}})-2L\epsilon\right\}\left(g(T^{i},x^{i})-g(T^{i^{*}},x^{i^{*}})+2L\epsilon\right)

(69)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{i^{*}}\left(g(T^{i^{*}},x^{i^{*}})-2L\epsilon\right)-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in\{i^{*}+1,\ldots,N\}:\;g(T^{i},x^{i})\geq g(T^{i^{*}},x^{i^{*}})-2L\epsilon}\left(g(T^{i},x^{i})-g(T^{i^{*}},x^{i^{*}})+2L\epsilon\right)

(70)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{i^{*}}\left(g(T^{i^{*}},x^{i^{*}})-2L\epsilon\right)-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in\{i^{*}+1,\ldots,N\}:\;g(T^{i},x^{i})\geq g(T^{i^{*}},x^{i^{*}})-2L\epsilon}2L\epsilon

(71)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{i^{*}}\left(g(T^{i^{*}},x^{i^{*}})-2L\epsilon\right)-\frac{N-i^{*}}{N}2L\epsilon

(72)

\displaystyle=\frac{i^{*}}{N}g(T^{i^{*}},x^{i^{*}})-2\epsilon L,

(73)

Indeed, (
65
) holds because
J^{\textnormal{\ref{prob:h_bar}}}
is greater than or equal to the optimal objective value of the optimization problem (

¯
H

-LB
) and because
b,w
is a feasible but possibly suboptimal solution for the optimization problem (

¯
H

-LB
). Lines (
66
), (
67
), and (
68
) follow from algebra. Line (
69
) follows from the fact that
\kappa_{0}^{i}<\cdots<\kappa^{i}_{L^{i}_{T^{i}}}
. Line (
70
) follows from rearranging terms. Line (
71
) follows from the fact that
g(T^{i},x^{i})-g(T^{i^{*}},x^{i^{*}})\leq 0
for all
i\in\{i^{*}+1,\ldots,N\}
. Lines (
72
) and (
73
) follow from algebra.

Since
i^{*}\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
was chosen arbitrarily, our proof of Claim
12
is complete.
\halmos

Claim 13

J^{\textnormal{\ref{prob:h_bar}}}\geq\frac{1}{\log N+1}\left(\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}g(T^{i},x^{i})\right)-2\epsilon L
.

Proof H.13

Proof of Claim
13
.
Assume without loss of generality that
g(T^{1},x^{1})\geq\cdots\geq g(T^{N},x^{N})
. In Claim
12
, we showed that

\displaystyle J^{\textnormal{\ref{prob:h_bar}}}\geq\max_{i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}}\frac{i}{N}g(T^{i},x^{i})-2\epsilon L.

For notational convenience, let us define
\gamma^{i}\triangleq\frac{i}{N}g(T^{i},x^{i})
for each sample path
i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
. With this notation, we observe from algebra that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\log N+1}\left(\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}g(T^{i},x^{i})\right)-2\epsilon L

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\log N+1}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{1}{i}\gamma^{i}\right)-2\epsilon L

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\log N+1}\left(\max_{i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}}\gamma^{i}\right)\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{1}{i}-2\epsilon L

\displaystyle\leq\max_{i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}}\gamma^{i}-2\epsilon L

\displaystyle=\max_{i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}}\frac{i}{N}g(T^{i},x^{i})-2\epsilon L

\displaystyle\leq J^{\textnormal{\ref{prob:h_bar}}},

where the last line follows from Claim
12
. This completes the proof of Claim
13
.

\halmos

Combining Claim
13
with Lemma
H.6
completes our proof of Proposition
4.13
.

\halmos

Appendix I
Additional Numerical Results

In this appendix, we present numerical results for additional parameter settings which were omitted from §
5.2
due to length considerations.
